Basic data propagation review

These records describe static possible behavior under the shared abstract runtime contract, not execution logs. complete means only that solving finished under the contract, not that the model actually observed this content. Default observations and explicit exceptions jointly constrain analysis. Fixed ordering or absence of a contract citation does not establish an observed runtime fact. Entry and exit states retain computation and audit evidence, rather than complete historical environments. An omitted binding does not establish deletion or an empty value.

Solver status: incomplete

Data · Operation records · Sink list

Select a D label to view its data version. Argument indexes belong to atomic operations, not original IR operands. Control slots determine member inclusion or observation and are not delivered payload. Candidate sets do not imply simultaneous occurrence; possible dependencies do not imply complete plaintext inclusion. This stage makes no risk, necessity or DOE judgment. Read scope, model observation, explicit selection and actual arguments are reviewed separately; tool boundaries do not imply networking.

      "content": "---\nname: google-search\ndescription: Search the web using Google Custom Search Engine (PSE). Use this when you need live information, documentation, or to research topics and the built-in web_search is unavailable.\n---\n\n# Google Search Skill\n\nThis skill allows OpenClaw agents to perform web searches via Google's Custom Search API (PSE).\n\n## Setup\n\n1.  **Google Cloud Console:** Create a project and enable the \"Custom Search API\".\n2.  **API Key:** Generate an API Key.\n3.  **Search Engine ID (CX):** Create a Programmable Search Engine at [cse.google.com](https://cse.google.com/cse/all), and get your CX ID.\n4.  **Environment:** Store your credentials in a `.env` file in your workspace:\n    ```\n    GOOGLE_API_KEY=your_key_here\n    GOOGLE_CSE_ID=your_cx_id_here\n    ```\n\n## Workflow\n... (rest of file)\n\n## Example Usage\n\n```bash\nGOOGLE_API_KEY=xxx GOOGLE_CSE_ID=yyy python3 skills/google-search/scripts/search.py \"OpenClaw documentation\"\n```\n",
      "path": "SKILL.md",
      "sha256": "d9600f3dc7af3761e2b2f421e05e78238f1f11d2e22cad0a35160bb1697c11a2"

    {
      "content": "import sys\nimport json\nimport requests\nimport os\n\ndef google_search(query, api_key, cse_id, num_results=5):\n    url = \"https://www.googleapis.com/customsearch/v1\"\n    params = {\n        'q': query,\n        'key': api_key,\n        'cx': cse_id,\n        'num': num_results\n    }\n    response = requests.get(url, params=params)\n    response.raise_for_status()\n    return response.json()\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    if len(sys.argv) < 2:\n        print(\"Usage: python google_search.py <query> [num_results]\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    query = sys.argv[1]\n    num_results = int(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 5\n    \n    # We will pass these via env vars for security\n    api_key = os.getenv(\"GOOGLE_API_KEY\")\n    cse_id = os.getenv(\"GOOGLE_CSE_ID\")\n\n    if not api_key or not cse_id:\n        print(\"Error: GOOGLE_API_KEY and GOOGLE_CSE_ID must be set in environment.\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    try:\n        results = google_search(query, api_key, cse_id, num_results)\n        print(json.dumps(results, indent=2))\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Error: {str(e)}\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n",

EM12

Boundary properties and defaults: access_scope is task, recipient, shared, or public; retention is task, persistent, or null when a receiving boundary's retention is not modeled. Storage and runtime-context locations require non-null retention. A task-internal tool requires supported local execution and task-internal receiving scope; explicitly nonlocal tools use recipient. Unspecified tool deployment conservatively retains an external recipient possibility, without inventing net_send or asserting measured remote execution. Runtime context defaults to task access and task retention; explicit cross-task memory or cross-subject sharing changes the respective property. Files default to task access and persistent retention; task-limited retention requires an explicit temporary-use and cleanup mechanism, not a temp-sounding name. Model, remote, and user boundaries default to recipient access, with null retention unless explicit retention is supported. A locally hosted model remains a content-processing recipient. Public access and sharing require source, code, interface, or supported contract evidence. Null retention does not establish no saving, logging, or future reuse; do not invent unmentioned backend persistence. These properties characterize static boundaries, not sensitivity, necessity, risk, or execution success.

Remote access defaults to recipient scope; backend retention is not established.

stdout · tool: process stdout

skill/scripts/search.py:20-38

print("Usage: python google_search.py <query> [num_results]") sys.exit(1) query = sys.argv[1] num_results = int(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 5 # We will pass these via env vars for security api_key = os.getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY") cse_id = os.getenv("GOOGLE_CSE_ID") if not api_key or not cse_id: print("Error: GOOGLE_API_KEY and GOOGLE_CSE_ID must be set in environment.") sys.exit(1) try: results = google_search(query, api_key, cse_id, num_results) print(json.dumps(results, indent=2)) except Exception as e: print(f"Error: {str(e)}")

print delivers the selected strings to process stdout. Its downstream consumer is unspecified.

EM06

Independent interaction boundaries: tool names and LLM participation do not prove network communication; sending verbs, recipient identifiers, and executor labels are not communication-mechanism evidence. Choose remote/net_send/net_receive only when the source, code, or interface establishes network transport; otherwise retain a known tool at tool/recipient/null under EM12; ordinary return does not prove user-facing output. Model observation, network delivery, and user output are separate boundaries. Preserving possible observation of a container does not broaden later API arguments: delivery must retain actual selected values, input order, result identities, and explicit prohibitions. Lack of a concrete getter or execution framework alone does not disable the declared defaults. Preserve calculable partial order; report a semantic failure only when a necessary relation cannot be represented or assessed.

A process output stream does not establish network transport, human-facing output or model observation.
